Here is where the pieces stand:

- **Black:** king g8, rook d2, knight g3, pawns b3, f7, h6, h7
- **White:** only a king on b1

**No mate in one works:**
- **Rd1+** fails because the white king escapes to b2.
- **b2?** would be stalemate. The white king would have no legal moves: a1 and c1 are hit by the pawn, a2 and c2 by the rook, and the pawn on b2 is defended by the rook.

**The plan:** bring the knight to d3 so it covers b2 (and c1). The rook stays on the 2nd rank, then drops to the 1st rank for mate. The b3 pawn covers a2 and c2.

**Ne4 is safe:** it starts the knight toward d3 via f2 or c5. The white king still has legal moves on a1 and c1, so there is no stalemate.

- b3, c4 and d4 are covered by the queen.

That leaves only b4, and Black can keep pressing for mate from there.
